segments each. This is because due to progressiveness all local configurations have to be resumed and all thread terminations occur after exactly
K
context switches. Each execution also corresponds to a thread type
t
, which can be determined by taking note of the global state and top of stack symbol at the start of each segment and the global state at the end of each segment of the execution. Since all executions end in the empty stack,
L_{t}\neq\varnothing
for all thread types
t
that occur on
\rho
in this way. Let us now construct from
\rho
an infinite run of
\mathcal{V}
starting from
c_{0}=(q_{0},\emptyset,\emptyset)
:

•

Start with the edge from (
1
) to create a token on the place corresponding to the initial stack symbol
\gamma_{0}
.

•

Go over all consecutive segments of
\rho
. If a segment is the first segment of an execution:

–

Start with an edge from (
2
) to remove a token from place
\gamma
, which is the top of stack symbol this segment starts with. Use as
g_{2}
the global state this segment starts with, as
g_{3}
the global state this segment ends in, and as
t
the thread type this segment’s execution corresponds to. This leads to a state of
\mathcal{V}
that contains the semi-linear set
s(t)
.

–

Next, take an edge from (
3
) to create a balloon whose contents characterize exactly the spawns made by this segment’s execution. This is possible because we kept track of
s(t)
in the state, and the appropriate balloon contents are in this semi-linear set by construction.

–

From here we need to deflate the balloon
|\Gamma|
times to transfer all the spawns from this segment to the places of
\mathcal{V}
. This is done using the edges from (
4
) for each
i
from
0
to
l-1
on the balloon places
(\gamma_{0},0)
to
(\gamma_{l-1},0)
. The
0
as the second component is because this is the first segment of an execution.

–

If this is not the last segment of its execution, we use an edge from (
5
) to perform the last deflation on the balloon place
(\gamma_{l},0)
and go to the global state that this segment ends in, using that state as
g_{2}
.

–

If it is the last segment of its execution we instead use an edge from (
6
) perform the same deflation, but go to a state
(g_{1},\bot,g_{2})
instead. From there we use the edge from (
7
) to burst the balloon and finally go to state
g_{2}
, which this segment ends in.

•

If a segment is not the first segment of its execution:

–

Start with an edge from (
8
) using as
g_{2}
the global state this segment starts with, as
g_{3}
the global state this segment ends in, and as
\bar{q}
the balloon state we ended in after handling the previous segment of this execution.

–

From here continue like for a first segment of an execution, deflating the balloon
|\Gamma|
times in the next few steps. Note that we operate on balloon places
\Gamma\times\{j\}
, where this is the
j
th segment within its execution, instead of
\Gamma\times\{0\}
.

Since each thread execution of
\rho
has exactly
K
segments, any balloon will eventually reach the
K
th segment while going over
\rho
. This results in an edge from (
7
) being used to burst the balloon. Therefore any balloon state occurring in a configuration of
\mathcal{V}
on the constructed run will be used for a deflate or burst later. Furthermore, each place
\gamma\in\Gamma
corresponds to a local configuration
(\gamma,0)
of
\mathcal{A}
, and we remove a token from it whenever we switch in a thread with that local configuration. Since such local configurations always have to be resumed on
\rho
, the corresponding places will always have tokens removed from them as well. Thus the handling of all balloons and places fulfils the conditions for a progressive run.

The constructed run of
\mathcal{V}
is also sound: For each segment of
\rho
it has a sequence of transitions that make the same state change as that segment, and also apply the same spawns. The latter is due to initially choosing the contents during the creation of a balloon correctly. A token representing the initial thread is also created in the beginning of the run. Since the transitions from one segment to the next require the same resumption rules
{\Delta_{\mathsf{r}}}
as in
\rho
(see edges (
2
) and (
8
)) these are also possible in
\mathcal{V}
.

For the
if direction
we do a very similar construction, but backwards. Starting with an infinite progressive run of
\mathcal{V}
from
c_{0}
, we decompose it into segments that end in a state in
G
. Afterwards we group those segments together by matching the local balloon configurations in the same way we did for local thread configurations before. Then by construction of
L_{t}
we know that there is a thread execution of type
t
that makes the same spawns in each segment as the group of one balloon does. Those executions will all terminate after
K
context switches, guaranteeing progressiveness, and we can interleave them in the same way as the balloon segments to form an infinite progressive run of
\mathcal{A}
.

Appendix C
Proofs for Section
5

Balloons with Identity

In order to prove Lemma
5.1
, we will work with runs which
contain a unique identity for every balloon i.e. with

balloons-with-id
. Formally,
a balloon-with-id is a tuple
(b,i)
where
b
is a balloon and
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
. A configuration-with-id
d
is a tuple
d=(q,\mathbf{m},\nu)

where

\nu
is a multiset of balloons-with-id. For a
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}=(Q,P,\Omega,\Phi,E)
, the edges in
E
define a transition relation on
configurations-with-id. For an edge
q\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}}q^{\prime}
, and
configurations-with-id

d=(q,\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n})
and
d^{\prime}=(q^{\prime},\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}^{\prime})
, we define

d\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}}d^{\prime}
:

•

If
\mathit{op}=\delta\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{P}
and

\mathbf{m}^{\prime}=\mathbf{m}+\delta
and
\mathbf{n}^{\prime}=\mathbf{n}
.

•

If
\mathit{op}=\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,S)
and

\mathbf{m}^{\prime}=\mathbf{m}
and
\mathbf{n}^{\prime}=\mathbf{n}+{[\![((\sigma,\mathbf{k}),i)]\!]}
for some

\mathbf{k}\in S
and
i\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
That is, we create a new balloon with state

\sigma
, multiset
\mathbf{k}
for some
\mathbf{k}\in S
and arbitrary id

i
.

•

If
\mathit{op}=\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)
and
there is a balloon-with-id
(b,i)=((\sigma,\mathbf{k}),i)\in\Omega\times{\mathbb{M}[\Phi]}
with
\mathbf{n}(b,i)\geq 1
and

\mathbf{m}^{\prime}=\mathbf{m}+\mathbf{k}(\pi)\cdot{[\![p]\!]}
and

\mathbf{n}^{\prime}=\mathbf{n}-{[\![(b,i)]\!]}+{[\![((\sigma^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}),i)]\!]}
,
where
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}(\pi)=0
and
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime})=\mathbf{k}(\pi^{\prime})
for all
\pi^{\prime}\in\Phi\setminus{\{\pi\}}
.
That is, we pick a balloon-with-id
((\sigma,\mathbf{k}),i)
from

\mathbf{n}
, transfer the contents in place
\pi
from
\mathbf{k}

to place
p
in
\mathbf{m}
, and update the state
\sigma
to
\sigma^{\prime}

while retaining its id.
Here we say the balloon-with-id
((\sigma,\mathbf{k}),i)
was

deflated
.

•

If
\mathit{op}=\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)
and
there is a balloon-with-id
(b,i)=((\sigma,\mathbf{k}),i)\in\Omega\times{\mathbb{M}[\Phi]}\times{\mathbb{N}}
with
\mathbf{n}(b,i)\geq 1
and

\mathbf{m}^{\prime}=\mathbf{m}
and
\mathbf{n}^{\prime}=\mathbf{n}\ominus{[\![(b,i)]\!]}
.
This means we pick some balloon-with-id
(b,i)
with state
\sigma

from our multiset
\mathbf{n}
of balloons-with-id and remove it,
making any tokens still contained in its balloon places disappear as well.
Here we say the balloon-with-id
(b,i)
is
burst
.

We often simply say ‘the balloon
i
’ was burst (or deflated) when
balloon
identities are unique.
A
run-with-id

\tau=d_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{2}\cdots
is a finite or infinite sequence of
configurations-with-id. We note that a
semiconfiguration-with-id is just a semiconfiguration since there are
no balloons.

Definition C.1.

We associate a
canonical run-with-id

\tau=d_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{2}\cdots
to any given run
\rho=c_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}c_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{2}}\cdots
as follows:

•

If
\mathit{op}_{i}
creates a balloon
b
in
\rho
, then it creates
(b,i)
in
\tau
. This implies that balloons are assigned unique id’s
since every operation creates only one balloon.

•

If
\mathit{op}_{i}
deflates (resp. bursts) a balloon
b
in
\rho
,
then it deflates (resp. bursts)
the balloon-with-id
(b,j)
in
\tau
where
j=min\{k\mid d_{i-1}.\nu(b,k)\geq 1\}
.

We observe that the multiset
d_{i}.\nu
of any configuration-with-id

d_{i}
in a canonical run-with-id
\tau
is infact a set. The set of
id’s in
\tau
is denoted
I(\tau)
. A collection of
balloons-with-id with the same id
i_{0}
may be viewed as a particular
balloon
which
undergoes a sequence of operations
\mathsf{seq}_{i_{1}}=\mathit{op}_{i_{0}},\mathit{op}_{i_{1}},\mathit{op}_{i_{2}},\cdots
.
The balloons
(b_{j},i_{0})

resulting from the operations
\mathit{op}_{i_{j}}
are associated with the id

i_{0}
and we will perform surgery on a run-with-id
\tau
resulting in
a modified run
\tau^{\prime}
by
replacing the balloon
(b_{1},i_{0})
at its point of inflation
\mathit{op}_{i_{0}}
by
(b^{\prime}_{1},i_{0})
in
\tau^{\prime}
, with the
implicit assumption that the sequence of operations
\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{i_{0}}
in
the modified run-with-id
\tau^{\prime}
now act on
(b^{\prime}_{1},i_{0})
instead. Note
that we use the notation
\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{i}
to represent the sequence of
operations on id
i
occuring in the modified run
\tau^{\prime}
.
The
sequences
\mathsf{seq}_{i_{0}}
and
\mathsf{seq}_{j_{0}}
are disjoint for
i_{0}\neq j_{0}
and thus the set of sequences
\{\mathsf{seq}_{i}\mid i\in\tau\}
form
a partition
of the indices
I_{B}(\tau)=\{i\mid\mathit{op}_{i}\text{ is a balloon
operation }\}
.
We will write
i.\sigma,i.\mathbf{k}
in short for
(b,i).\sigma
and
(b,i).\mathbf{k}
where
(b,i)
is created by
\mathit{op}_{i}
. The linear set
used to create a balloon with the id
i
is denoted
L_{i}
.
A run-with-id
\tau

corresponds
to a run
\rho
if
\rho
is
obtained from
\tau
by removing id’s. For a balloon
b
in
\rho
, the
set
\{(b,i)\mid i\in I(\tau)\}
of balloons-with-id in a corresponding

\tau
is called the

balloon class
of
b
.

The fact that reachability remains preserved whether considering runs
or runs-with-id immediately follows from the definition of a
canonical run-with-id:

Proposition C.2.

Given
\mathcal{V}
and its semiconfigurations
s_{1},s_{2}
, there exists a run

\rho=s_{1}\xrightarrow{*}s_{2}
of
\mathcal{V}
iff there exists a
corresponding run-with-id

\tau=s_{1}\xrightarrow{*}s_{2}
.

The fact that progressivenss is also preserved is also not difficult
to see. We first define the obvious notion of progressiveness for a
run-with-id.

Definition C.3.

A progressive run-with-id
\tau=d_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{2}\cdots
is one such that:

•

for every
balloon-with-id
(b,i)\in d_{j}
for some
d_{j}\in\tau
, there exists

d_{k}
with
k>j
such that
d_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{k}}d_{k+1}
where

\mathit{op}_{k}
is a deflate or
burst operation on id
i
, and

•

for every
p\in P
if
d_{i}.\mathbf{m}(p)>0
then there exists

j>i
such that
d_{j}\xrightarrow{\delta}d_{j+1}
where
\delta(p)<0
.

The following proposition follows from the above definition.

Proposition C.4.

In a progressive run-with-id
\tau
, for any id
i
, one of the
following is true:

(1)

\mathsf{seq}_{i}
is finite and the final operation in
\mathsf{seq}_{i}
is a
burst operation, or

(2)

\mathsf{seq}_{i}
is infinite.

If
\mathsf{seq}_{i}
is finite for all
i
, we say ‘every balloon is burst in

\tau
’.
The existence of progressive runs and progressive runs-with-id also
coincide.

Proposition C.5.

For any given
\mathcal{V}
and its semiconfiguration
s
, there exists a
progressive run
\rho
from
s
iff there exists a corresponding
progressive run-with-id
\tau^{\prime}
from
s
.

Proof.

Clearly a progressive run
\rho
can be obtained from a progressive
run-with-id
\tau
by id-removal. For the converse direction, consider
the canonical
\tau=d_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{2}\cdots

corresponding to
\rho=c_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}c_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{2}}c_{2}\cdots
.
The canonical
\tau
satisfies all progressiveness conditions
except for one special case: a balloon
b
in
\rho
which undergoes
an infinite sequence of trivial deflates at
j_{1},j_{2},\cdots

may result in
\tau
which contains multiple balloons-with-id in the
balloon class of
b
at every configuration
d_{j_{1}},d_{j_{2}},\cdots
.
By construction,
\tau
always
chooses the least id and hence

\tau
is not progressive for other id’s in the balloon
class of
b
.
The progressive
\tau^{\prime}
is obtained by modifying
\tau
to ‘dovetail’
through all possible choices in the balloon class of
b
(i.e. if
the id’s in the balloon class are
i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}\cdots
, then the
choices made are
i_{1},i_{1},i_{2},i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{1}\cdots
). We note
that
\tau^{\prime}
agrees with
\tau
on all inflate and burst operations.

We now consider the two properties assumed in the proof of Lemma

5.1
, namely that of being zero-base and being typed
and show that it is possible to construct a
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
with these
properties from a given
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}
while preserving reachability
and progressiveness.

Balloon Types and Surgeries on Balloons

A deflate operation transferring a
non-zero number of tokens is called a
non-trivial
deflate,
otherwise it is a trivial deflate. Clearly, a non-trivial deflate
moving tokens from a balloon place
\pi
must be the first deflate
which transfers tokens from
\pi
, motivating the following
definition.
For an id
i
, define

\displaystyle\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}=

\displaystyle\{(\pi,p,j)\mid\exists\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\Omega\;\exists j\in\mathsf{seq}_{i}\colon\mathit{op}_{j}=\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)\text{ and }

\displaystyle\forall\sigma^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\Omega\;\forall p^{\prime}\in P\;\forall j^{\prime}\in\mathsf{seq}_{i},j^{\prime}<j\colon\mathit{op}_{j^{\prime}}\neq\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime\prime},\pi,p^{\prime})\}

We write
i<_{t}j
for id’s
i,j
if
i<j
,
L_{i}=L_{j}
where
L_{i}

(resp.
L_{j}
) is
the linear set used to inflate balloon
i
(resp.
j
) and for every
(\pi,p)\in\Phi\times P
, there exists
k\in\mathsf{seq}_{i}
such that
(\pi,p,k)\in\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}
iff there exists
k^{\prime}\in\mathsf{seq}_{j}
with
k^{\prime}>k
such that
(\pi,p,k^{\prime})\in\mathsf{typeseq}_{j}
.

A
deflate-sequence

S=(\pi_{1},p_{1}),(\pi_{2},{\mathsf{p}}_{2}),\cdots,(\pi_{n},p_{n})
is a finite sequence of elements from
\Phi\times P

which satisfies the property that for all
i,j\in[1,n]
, we have

\pi_{i}\neq\pi_{j}
. We write
n=|S|
and also write
\mathsf{set}(S)

for
the set of tuples
(\pi_{i},p_{i})
obtained from
S
by ignoring the
order. A marked deflate-sequence

M=(S,i)
is a tuple
consisting of a deflate-sequence
S
and
i\in[0,\cdots,|S|]
. We
write
M.S
and
M.i
for the two components of
M
. If
M^{\prime}=(S,i+1)

and
M=(S,i)
, we write
M^{\prime}=M+1
. The
set of all marked deflate-sequences is denoted by
\mathcal{M}
.

Let
\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}\hskip-3.0pt\downharpoonright\;=(\pi_{1},p_{1}),(\pi_{2},p_{2}),\cdots,(\pi_{n},p_{n})
be
the deflate-sequence obtained by
projecting
\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}
to the first two
components, in increasing order of the third component. We write

k=\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}(j)
if
(\pi_{j},p_{j},k)\in\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}
.
We write
i\sim_{t}j
if
\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}\hskip-3.0pt\downharpoonright\;=\mathsf{typeseq}_{j}\hskip-3.0pt\downharpoonright
and
L_{i}=L_{j}
. A tuple
t=(L_{i},\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}\hskip-3.0pt\downharpoonright)
is called a
type
and only depends on
\mathcal{V}
.
The set of finitely many types associated with
\mathcal{V}
is denoted
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{V}}
; we drop the subscript when
\mathcal{V}
is clear from the context.

Given any
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}=(Q,P,\Omega,\Phi,E_{p}\cup E_{n}\cup E_{d}\cup E_{b})
,
its
typed extension

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=(Q,P,\Omega^{\prime},\Phi,E_{p}\cup E^{\prime}_{n}\cup E^{\prime}_{d}\cup E^{\prime}_{b})

is given by
\Omega^{\prime}=\Omega\times\mathcal{M}
and set of balloon edges is given
as:

(1)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L)}q^{\prime}
in
E_{n}
, for
each
M\in\mathcal{M}
such that
M.i=0
, add

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}((\sigma,M),L)}q^{\prime}
to
E^{\prime}_{n}
,

(2)

for each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}

in
E_{d}
and for each
M\in\mathcal{M}
such that
(\pi,p)=(\pi_{j},p_{j})\in M
for some
j\in[1,\cdots,|M.S|]

(a)

if
j\leq M.i
then add

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\sigma,M),(\sigma^{\prime},M),\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
to

E^{\prime}_{d}
,

(b)

else if
j=(M.i)+1
then add

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\sigma,M),(\sigma^{\prime},M+1),\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
to

E^{\prime}_{d}
, and

(3)

for each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)}q^{\prime}
in
E_{b}
and
for each
M\in\mathcal{M}
with
M.i=|M.S|
, add

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma,M)}q^{\prime}
to
E^{\prime}_{b}
.

Lemma C.6.

Given any
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}
and two semiconfigurations
s_{1},s_{2}

of
\mathcal{V}
,
its typed extension
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
satisfies the following
properties:

(1)

\mathcal{V}
has a progressive run starting from
s_{1}
iff
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}

has a progressive run starting from
s_{1}
and

(2)

(\mathcal{V},s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathsf{REACH}
iff
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathsf{REACH}
.

Proof.

Given a run
\rho^{\prime}=c^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{1}}c^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{2}}\cdots
of

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
starting from a semiconfiguration
c^{\prime}_{0}
, it is clear that there
exists a run
\rho=c^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}c_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{2}}\cdots
of
\mathcal{V}
where for each
k\geq 1
,

•

if
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{k}\in E_{p}
then
\mathit{op}_{k}=\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{k}
,

•

else if
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{k}
creates a balloon
b^{\prime}
with state
(\sigma,M)
, then
\mathit{op}_{k}
creates a
balloon
b
such that
b.\mathbf{k}=b^{\prime}.\mathbf{k}
and
b.\sigma=\sigma
,

•

otherwise
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{k}
is a deflate or burst operation on a
balloon
b^{\prime}
with state
(\sigma,M)
and
\mathit{op}_{k}

applies the corresponding deflate or burst operation to a balloon

b
with
b.\mathbf{k}=b^{\prime}.\mathbf{k}
and
b.\sigma=\sigma
.

By induction on length of the run, we see that
c_{k}.\mathbf{m}=c^{\prime}_{k}.\mathbf{m}
for
each
k\geq 0
and further, there is a bijection between
c_{k}.\nu

and
c^{\prime}_{k}.\nu
which preserves the balloon contents with the
state of a balloon in
c_{k}
obtained by projecting the state of the
corresponding balloon to the first component. If
\rho^{\prime}
is a
finite run ending in a semiconfiguration, this implies that
\rho

also ends in the same semiconfiguration. Since
\rho^{\prime}
is progressive
with respect to balloon states of the form
(\sigma,M)
, clearly

\rho
is progressive with respect to the balloon states
\sigma
.

For the converse direction, we argue using runs-with-id.
Let
\tau=d_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{2}}\cdots
be the
canonical
run-with-id of
\mathcal{V}
starting from a semiconfiguration
d_{0}
for a
given
\rho
of
\mathcal{V}
. We
define the run-with-id
\tau^{\prime}=d_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{2}}\cdots
of

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
where for each
k\geq 1
,

•

if
\mathit{op}_{k}\in E_{p}
then
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{k}=\mathit{op}_{k}
,

•

if
\mathit{op}_{k}\in E_{n}
creates
(b,i)
then
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{k}
creates
(b^{\prime},i)
where
b^{\prime}.\sigma=(b.\sigma,M)
with
M=(\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}\downharpoonright,0)
and
b^{\prime}.\mathbf{k}=b.\mathbf{k}
, and

•

if
\mathit{op}_{k}\in E_{d}\cup E_{b}
and
\mathit{op}_{k}\in\mathsf{seq}_{i}
for some id

i
, then
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{i}
. Here
\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{i}
(resp.
\mathsf{seq}_{i}
)
refers to the
sequence of operations on id
i
in
\tau^{\prime}
(resp.
\tau
).

If
\tau
is a finite run between semiconfigurations
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
,
then so is

\tau^{\prime}
and thus we have (2) of the lemma.
If
\tau
is a
progressive run-with-id from
s_{1}
then so is

\tau^{\prime}
and by Proposition
C.5
, we conclude (1)
of the lemma.

In the remainder of this section, we assume that any
\mathsf{VASSB}
is
typed.

A
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}
is said to be
zero-base
if every linear set in

\mathcal{V}
has base vector
\emptyset
.

Lemma C.7.

Given any
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}
, we can construct a zero-base
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
such that for any two semiconfigurations
s_{1},s_{2}
of
\mathcal{V}
,

(1)

\mathcal{V}
has a progressive run starting from
s_{1}
iff
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}

has a progressive run starting from
s_{1}
and

(2)

(\mathcal{V},s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathsf{REACH}
iff
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathsf{REACH}
.

Proof.

We may assume that there exists a unique linear set
L_{\sigma}
associated with any given balloon state
\sigma
of
\mathcal{V}
such
that any
balloon
b
inflated with state
\sigma
has
b.\mathbf{k}\in L_{\sigma}
,
by applying the following modification to
\mathcal{V}
.
Let
\mathcal{L}
be the finite set of linear sets used in
\mathcal{V}
. We
replace the set of balloon states
\Omega
by
the cartesian product
\Omega\times\mathcal{L}
and for each inflate
operation
\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L)
we use
\mathsf{inflate}((\sigma,L),L)
in the modified

\mathsf{VASSB}
. This modification is easily seen to preserve
progressiveness and reachability. We also assume that the given

\mathcal{V}
is typed and instead of writing balloon states as tuples
(\sigma,M)
, we assume that every state
\sigma
comes with an
associated
M_{\sigma}
.

Let
\mathcal{V}=(Q,P,\Omega,\Phi,E_{p}\cup E_{n}\cup E_{d}\cup E_{b})
and
\mathbf{b}_{\sigma}
be the base vector of a
balloon state
\sigma\in\Omega
.

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=(Q^{\prime},P,\Omega^{\prime},\Phi,E^{\prime}_{p}\cup E^{\prime}_{n}\cup E^{\prime}_{d}\cup E_{b})

is constructed from
\mathcal{V}
as follows:

(1)

Q^{\prime}=Q\cup E_{d}\times P
,

(2)

\Omega^{\prime}=\Omega
contains a state
\sigma^{\prime}
for each

\sigma
such that

L_{\sigma^{\prime}}
is obtained from
L_{\sigma}
by removing the base
vector
\mathbf{b}_{\sigma}
,

(3)

E^{\prime}_{p}=E_{p}\cup E^{\prime\prime}_{p}
, where
E^{\prime\prime}_{p}
contains for each edge

e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
in
E_{d}
with
M_{\sigma_{1}}\neq M_{\sigma_{2}}
, the edge

(e,p)\xrightarrow{\delta}q^{\prime}
where
\delta(p)=\mathbf{b}_{\sigma}(p)
and
\delta(p^{\prime})=0
for
p^{\prime}\neq p
,

(4)

E^{\prime}_{n}
contains an edge
q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma^{\prime},L_{\sigma^{\prime}})}q^{\prime}

for
each edge
q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L_{\sigma})}q^{\prime}
in
E_{n}
, and

(5)

E^{\prime}_{d}
contains the following edges for each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
in
E_{d}
:

(a)

if
M_{\sigma_{1}}\neq M_{\sigma_{2}}
then we add
q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma^{\prime}_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{2},\pi,p)}(e,p)
to
E^{\prime}_{d}
,

(b)

else we add
q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma^{\prime}_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{2},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
to
E^{\prime}_{d}
.

Given a finite run
\rho=c_{0}\xrightarrow{}c_{1}\xrightarrow{}c_{2}\cdots c_{k}

of
\mathcal{V}
between semiconfigurations,
there exists a finite run
\rho^{\prime}=c^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{*}c^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{*}c^{\prime}_{2}\cdots c^{\prime}_{k}

where for
every
\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L)

operation
in

\rho
, we perform a
\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma^{\prime},L)
operation in
\rho^{\prime}
, creating
a
balloon which is identical except for the base vector. The missing
base
vector tokens are then transferred to the appropriate place via edges
of the form
(e,p)\xrightarrow{\delta}q^{\prime}
from (2). Note that the
extra base-vector tokens are only added during non-trivial deflates,
which is information that can be obtained from the type information in

M_{\sigma}
.

Conversely if

\rho^{\prime}
is a a finite run of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
between semiconfigurations of

\mathcal{V}
, we
observe that the states use to add the missing tokens are sandwiched
between the states of a
\mathsf{deflate}
operation and we obtain a run

\rho
from
\rho^{\prime}
by replacing the
\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma^{\prime},L)
operations by

\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L)
operations and replacing every chain of edges
q\rightarrow(e,p)\rightarrow q^{\prime}
by the corresponding
\mathsf{deflate}

edge
q\rightarrow q^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{V}
. Thus reachability is preserved. It is
easy to see that the
construction also preserves progressiveness since any infinite run of

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}

can also be decomposed into segments which are runs between
semiconfigurations of
\mathcal{V}
.

Henceforth we assume that any
\mathsf{VASSB}
is zero-base.

Token-shifting Surgery

Fix a run-with-id
\tau=d_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{2}}d_{2}\cdots
of a zero-base
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}
. Let
i
be
an id and
I
a finite set of id’s with the property that for each
j\in I

we have
i<_{t}j
. Let
N=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in K}\mathbf{k}
where
K=\{\mathbf{k}\mid\exists j\in I,\mathit{op}_{j}\text{ creates balloon }j\text{ with }\mathbf{k}=j.\mathbf{k}\}
.
The token-shifting surgery
\mathcal{S}_{i\leftarrow I}

on
\tau
yielding
\mathcal{S}_{i\leftarrow I}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}=d^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{1}}d^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{2}}d^{\prime}_{2}\cdots
is defined as, for each
k\geq 1
:

(1)

If
k\not\in I\cup\{i\}
, then
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{k}=\mathit{op}_{k}
,

(2)

else if
k=i
then if
\mathit{op}_{i}
creates
(b,i)
then
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i}

creates
(b^{\prime},i)
where
b^{\prime}.\sigma=b.\sigma
and
b^{\prime}.\mathbf{k}=b.\mathbf{k}+N
,

(3)

else
k\in I
and if
\mathit{op}_{k}
creates
(b_{k},k)
then
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{k}

creates
(b^{\prime},k)
where
b^{\prime}.\sigma=b.\sigma
and
b^{\prime}.\mathbf{k}=\emptyset
.

In other words, the only difference between
\tau^{\prime}
and
\tau
is the
content of balloons created at
I\cup\{i\}
and the resulting
changes in the configurations.

Proposition C.8.

For any
\tau=d_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{2}}d_{2}\cdots
and any
\mathcal{S}_{i\leftarrow I}
, the run
\mathcal{S}_{i\leftarrow I}(\tau)=\tau^{\prime}=d^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{1}}d^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{2}}d^{\prime}_{2}\cdots
is a valid run. Further, if
\tau
is a
progressive run from
s
then so is
\mathcal{S}_{i\leftarrow I}(\tau)
. If
\tau=s\xrightarrow{*}s^{\prime}
is a finite run
between two semiconfigurations, then
\mathcal{S}_{i\leftarrow I}(\tau)
is
also a run from
s
to
s^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The fact that
\mathcal{V}
is zero-base ensures that
b^{\prime}.\mathbf{k}
as defined
in (2)
belongs to the linear set
L_{i}
. The condition
i<_{t}j
for each
j\in I
implies that the balloon
i
makes its non-trivial deflates
before any of the balloons with an id from
I
. This implies that for
each
k\geq 1
we have
d_{k}.\mathbf{m}\leq d^{\prime}_{k}.\mathbf{m}
since the total
number of tokens transferred from
\pi
to
p
for any
\pi\in\Phi,p\in P
in
\tau^{\prime}
remains greater than that in
\tau
for
every prefix
\tau[0,k]
. By monotonicity, every operation remains
enabled in
\tau^{\prime}
. Progressiveness is preserved since the only
change is in the content of some of the newly created balloons. Since
the total number of tokens transferred in the entire run is the same
in
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
for each
(\pi,p)
in a finite run
between two semiconfigurations
s,s^{\prime}
, and there are no balloons in

s^{\prime}
, this implies that
\tau^{\prime}
reaches the same semiconfiguration

s^{\prime}
.

C.1.
Proof of Lemma
5.1

We have now introduced all of the machinery required to prove Lemma

5.1
. We will construct
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
from
\mathcal{V}
such that
the following are
equivalent:

(S1)

there exists a progressive run-with-id
\tau=s\rightarrow d_{1}\rightarrow d_{2}\cdots
of
\mathcal{V}
,

(S2)

there exists a progressive run-with-id
\tau^{\prime}=s\rightarrow d^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow d^{\prime}_{2}\cdots
of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
which bursts every balloon, and

(S3)

there exists an

A,B
-witness

\rho^{\prime}_{A,B}=s\xrightarrow{*}c\xrightarrow{*}c^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
for some
A\subseteq P^{\prime},B\subseteq\Omega^{\prime}
.

We will first show (S1) iff (S2) and then show (S2) iff (S3).
By Lemmas
C.7
and
C.6
we assume
that
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}
is zero-base and typed, and
that every
\sigma\in\Omega

comes with an associated marked deflate-sequence
M_{\sigma}
and
do not write balloon states explicitly as
(\sigma,M_{\sigma})
. From

\mathcal{V}=(Q,P,\Omega,\Phi,E_{p}\cup E_{n}\cup E_{d}\cup E_{b})
, we
construct

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=(Q^{\prime},P^{\prime},\Omega,\Phi,(E_{p}\cup E^{\prime\prime}_{p})\cup E_{n}\cup E^{\prime}_{d}\cup E^{\prime}_{b})
as
follows:

•

Q^{\prime}=Q\cup(E\times\{0,1\})
,

•

P^{\prime}=P\cup(\Omega\times\{0,1\})
,

•

E^{\prime}
is defined as follows:

(1)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
in
E_{d}
where
(\pi,p)=(\pi_{j},p_{j})\in M_{\sigma}
,

(a)

if
M_{\sigma}.i\leq|M_{\sigma}.S|-1
then
add,

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
to
E^{\prime}_{d}
,

(b)

else if
M_{\sigma}.i=|M_{\sigma}.S|
,

(i)

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
to
E^{\prime}_{d}
,

(ii)

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)}(e,0)

to
E^{\prime}_{d}
,

(iii)

(e,0)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma^{\prime})}(e,1)

to
E^{\prime}_{b}
,

(iv)

(e,1)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{\prime+}}q^{\prime}
to
E^{\prime\prime}_{p}
.

(v)

if
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
, for
(i,j)\in\{(0,1),(1,0)\}
add
q\xrightarrow{(\sigma,i)^{+}(\sigma,j)^{-}}q^{\prime}
to

E^{\prime\prime}_{p}
,

(vi)

else for each
i,j\in\{0,1\}
add
q\xrightarrow{(\sigma,i)^{-}(\sigma^{\prime},j)^{+}}q^{\prime}
to
E^{\prime\prime}_{p}

(2)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)}q^{\prime}
in
E_{b}
, add
q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)}q^{\prime}
to
E^{\prime}_{b}
.

For
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathsf{seq}_{i}
, we write
k\lessdot_{i}k^{\prime}
to denote the
successor relation in
\mathsf{seq}_{i}
.
Let
m
be the smallest id in
\tau
such
that
\mathsf{seq}_{m}
is infinite.
Since there can be at most finitely many indices in
\mathsf{seq}_{m}
where a
non-trivial deflate occurs, there exists
n\in\mathsf{seq}_{m}

which is the last such index.
Intuitively,
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
uses the edges in (1 b ii) to (1 b vi) to burst the
balloon
with id
m
at operation
n
where the last non-trivial deflate occurs
and
uses a
\mathsf{VASS}
token to simulate the effect of the infinite suffix of

\mathsf{seq}_{m}
which performs only trivial deflates. We use the notation

p^{+}
(resp.
p^{-}
) to indicate that the place
p
is incremented
(resp. decremented).
For example, for
p_{1},p_{2}\in P
, we write
p_{1}^{+}
,
p_{2}^{-}
, and
p_{1}^{-}p_{2}^{+}
for the vectors
mapping

p_{1}
to
+1
and all other places to
0
,

p_{2}
to
-1
and all other places to
0
,
and
p_{1}
to
-1
,
p_{2}
to
+1
, and all other places to
0
, respectively.

From the given progressive
\tau=s\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{2}}d_{2}\cdots
of
\mathcal{V}
, we construct a
progressive
\tau^{\prime}_{1}=d^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{*}d^{\prime}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{*}d^{\prime}_{i_{2}}\cdots
for
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
. We set
d^{\prime}_{0}=s
and
for every
k
, the run
d^{\prime}_{i_{k-1}}\xrightarrow{*}d^{\prime}_{i_{k}}
is defined
as
follows:

(I)

If
k\not\in\mathsf{seq}_{m}
or
k<n
then

(a)

if
\mathit{op}_{k}=\delta
then
i_{k}=i_{k-1}+1
and
d^{\prime}_{i_{k-1}}\xrightarrow{\delta}d^{\prime}_{i_{k}}
,

(b)

if
\mathit{op}_{k}=\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L)
creates balloon
(b,k)
,
then
i_{k}=i_{k-1}+1
and

d^{\prime}_{i_{k-1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L)}d^{\prime}_{i_{k}}

creates
(b^{\prime},i_{k})
with
b^{\prime}.\mathbf{k}=b.\mathbf{k}
,

(c)

else if
\mathit{op}_{k}=\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)
deflates balloon
k^{\prime}
, then
i_{k}=i_{k-1}+1
and

d^{\prime}_{i_{k-1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)}d^{\prime}_{i_{k}}

deflates balloon
i_{k^{\prime}}
,

(d)

else
\mathit{op}_{k}=\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)
bursts balloon
k^{\prime}
, then
i_{k}=i_{k-1}+1
and

d^{\prime}_{i_{k-1}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)}d^{\prime}_{i_{k}}

bursts balloon
i_{k^{\prime}}
,

(II)

otherwise we have
k\in\mathsf{seq}_{m}
and
k\geq n
, in which case,

(a)

if
k=n
and
\mathit{op}_{k}=\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)

deflates
m
,
then
i_{n}=i_{n-1}+3
and

d^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}+1}}d^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}+1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}+2}}d^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}+2}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}+3}}d^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}+3}
where

•

\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}+1}=\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)
deflates
balloon
i_{m}
where

n^{\prime}\lessdot_{m}n
,

•

\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}+2}=\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)
, and

•

\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{n-1}+3}=(\sigma,0)^{+}
,

(b)

else if
k>n
and
\mathit{op}_{k}=\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)
then

i_{k}=i_{k-1}+1
and

(i)

if
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
and
\exists k^{\prime}<_{m}k
such that

\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{k^{\prime}}}=(\sigma,1)^{+}(\sigma,0)^{-}
then

\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{k}}=(\sigma,0)^{+}(\sigma,1)^{-}
else

\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{k}}=(\sigma,1)^{+}(\sigma,0)^{-}
,

(ii)

else if
\sigma\neq\sigma^{\prime}
and
k^{\prime}\lessdot_{m}k
is
such that

\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{k^{\prime}}}=(\sigma,0)^{+}(\sigma^{\prime},0)^{-}

then

\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{k}}=(\sigma,0)^{+}(\sigma^{\prime},0)^{-}
else

\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i_{k}}=(\sigma,1)^{+}(\sigma^{\prime},1)^{-}
.

We repeat the construction starting with
\tau_{1}
, picking the next
higher index
m_{1}>m
such that
\mathsf{seq}(i_{1})
is infinite and obtain a
sequence of runs
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3}\cdots
which agree on
increasingly larger prefixes (since the id’s are chosen in order).
Thus we may define
\tau^{\prime}
as the limit of this sequence having the
property that every balloon is burst in
\tau^{\prime}
. This implies that

\tau^{\prime}
satisfies the progressiveness conditions for all places
p\in P
and all balloons. Our choices in (II b i) and (II b ii) imply that
\tau^{\prime}
also
satisfies progressiveness conditions for the places
\Omega\times\{0,1\}
.

Conversely, consider an arbitrary progressive run-with-id
\tau^{\prime}
of

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}

which bursts all balloons. It may
be decomposed as

\tau^{\prime}=d^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{*}d^{\prime}_{i_{1}}\xrightarrow{*}d^{\prime}_{i_{2}}\cdots
where each segment
d^{\prime}_{i_{k-1}}\xrightarrow{*}d^{\prime}_{i_{k}}
is either an edge from
E_{p}
,
(1 a),(1 b i),(1 b v),(1 b vi) or (2), or a sequence
of edges
(1 b ii,1 b iii,1 b iv). Choosing the
balloon with least index

m
such that
there exists a burst operation (1 b iii), we know there exists an
infinite sequence of corresponding
token transfers (1 b v,1 b vi) by progressiveness. By construction,
there
exist trivial deflates (1 b i) corresponding to these token transfers
and by replacing the transfers by (1 b i) edges, we obtain
\tau^{\prime}_{1}
.

Repeating the construction with
\tau^{\prime}_{1}
we obtain a sequence of runs

\tau^{\prime}_{1},\tau^{\prime}_{2},\cdots
which agree on increasingly larger prefixes
and
we obtain a progressive run
\tau^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
as the limit of these
runs. Since no edges from (1 b ii) to (1 b vi) exist in
\tau^{\prime\prime}
, this
implies that
\tau^{\prime\prime}
is infact a progressive
\tau
of
\mathcal{V}
. This
concludes the proof that (S1) iff (S2).

We now take up (S2) iff (S3), beginning with the only-if direction.
Let
\tau^{\prime}=d^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{}d^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{}d^{\prime}_{2}\cdots
be a progressive run of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
which bursts every
balloon. We explain the idea behind constructing the
required witness for (S3):
We pick a set of id’s
I_{1}
which contains one
balloon for each
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}
. We then inductively pick sets
I_{k}

for each
k>1
such that all balloons in
I_{k-1}
have been burst
before the creation of any balloon in
I_{k}
. This divides
\tau^{\prime}
into
segments such that the tokens of all balloons created in the
k^{th}

segment excluding those from
I_{k}
can be shifted to the balloons in

I_{k-1}
. In particular, this creates a infinite sequence of
configurations which contain only empty balloons, which can be used to
construct the required witness. Recall that
I(\tau)
denotes the
set of id’s
in
\tau
while
I_{B}(\tau)
denotes the indices of the operations in

\tau
which are balloons operations.
Formally, for each
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}
we define

i_{1,\sigma^{\prime}}=\mathsf{min}\{i\in I(\tau^{\prime})\mid\mathit{op}_{i}=\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma^{\prime},L_{\sigma^{\prime}})\}
,

I_{1}=\{i_{1,\sigma^{\prime}}\mid\sigma^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}\}
and

B_{1}=\{j_{1,\sigma^{\prime}}\in I_{B}(\tau^{\prime})\mid\exists i_{1,\sigma^{\prime}}\in I_{1},\mathit{op}_{j_{1,\sigma^{\prime}}}=\mathsf{burst}(\sigma^{\prime}),j_{1,\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathsf{seq}_{i_{1,\sigma^{\prime}}}\}
.

Inductively, we define for

k>1
:

i_{k,\sigma^{\prime}}=\mathsf{min}\{l\in I(\tau^{\prime})\mid\mathit{op}_{l}=\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma^{\prime},L_{\sigma^{\prime}}),l>\mathsf{max}(B_{k-1})\}
,

I_{k}=\{i_{k,\sigma^{\prime}}\mid\sigma^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}\}
and

B_{k}=\{j_{k,\sigma^{\prime}}\in I_{B}(\tau^{\prime})\mid\exists i_{k,\sigma^{\prime}}\in I_{k},\mathit{op}_{j_{k,\sigma^{\prime}}}=\mathsf{burst}(\sigma^{\prime}),j_{k,\sigma^{\prime}}\in\mathsf{seq}_{i_{k,\sigma^{\prime}}}\}
.

We also define, for
k\geq 1
,

I_{k,k+1,\sigma^{\prime}}=\{l\in I(\tau^{\prime})\mid l\not\in I_{k+1},\mathit{op}_{l}=\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma^{\prime},L_{\sigma^{\prime}})\text{ and }\mathsf{max}(B_{k})<l<\mathsf{max}(B_{k+1})
and

I_{k,k+1}=\bigcup_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}}I_{k,k+1,\sigma^{\prime}}
.

We note that by construction, for each
k\geq 1
, the id
i_{k,\sigma^{\prime}}

and the set of id’s
I_{k,k+1,\sigma^{\prime}}
satisfy the
requirements for a token-shifting
surgery. Applying the surgeries
\mathcal{S}_{i_{k,\sigma^{\prime}}\leftarrow I_{k,k+1,\sigma^{\prime}}}
to
\tau^{\prime}
, we obtain
\tau^{\prime\prime}=d^{\prime\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{}d^{\prime\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{}d^{\prime\prime}_{2}\cdots

which bursts every balloon. Therefore there exists
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
such
that for
any id
i
satisfying
i<\mathsf{max}(B_{1})
, balloon
i
is burst before

d^{\prime\prime}_{n}
. By construction, for every
k>n
the configuration
d^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{max}(B_{k})}
contains only empty balloons i.e. for every
(b,j)
such
that
d^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{max}(B_{k})}.\nu(b,j)=1
, we have
(b,j).\mathbf{k}=\emptyset
.

Let
S=d^{\prime\prime}_{j_{1}},d^{\prime\prime}_{j_{2}},\cdots
be the infinite sequence of
configurations in
\tau^{\prime\prime}
such that they only contain empty
balloons i.e. satisfy condition (3) of an
A,B
-witness. There exists
an infinite subsequence
S^{\prime}=d^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1}},d^{\prime\prime}_{k_{2}},\cdots
of
S
such that the corresponding id-removals

c^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1}},c^{\prime\prime}_{k_{2}},\cdots
satisfy
c^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1}}\leq c^{\prime\prime}_{k_{2}}\leq\cdots
due to the fact that
\leq
is a
Well-Quasi-Order. Further, by the Pigeonhole principle, we may assume
that they all have the same set of occupied places and same set of
balloon states
B
, thus satisfying condition (5) of an

A,B
-witness.
We pick
A=P_{\infty}={\{p\in P\mid\forall i\geq 0\exists j>i\colon d^{\prime\prime}_{j}.\mathbf{m}(p)>0\}}
, ensuring that
\mathsf{supp}(c^{\prime\prime}_{k_{l}})\subseteq A
as required. By progressiveness, there
exists

k_{m},k_{n}
with
k_{m}<k_{n}
such that conditions (2) and (4) of an

A,B
-witness are satisfied for
c_{k_{m}}
and
c_{k_{n}}
. Thus the run

\rho^{\prime\prime}=c_{0}\xrightarrow{*}c^{\prime\prime}_{k_{m}}\xrightarrow{*}c^{\prime\prime}_{k_{n}}
is the required
A,B
-witness.

Conversely, given an
A,B
-witness
\rho^{\prime\prime}=c_{0}\xrightarrow{*}c\xrightarrow{*}c^{\prime}
let
\rho^{\prime}=c_{0}\xrightarrow{*}c_{1}\xrightarrow{*}c_{2}\xrightarrow{*}c_{3}\cdots
be its ‘unrolling’ where

c_{1}=c,c_{2}=c^{\prime}
and for each
k\geq 3
, the configuration
c_{k}
is
obtained from
c_{k-1}
by applying the sequence of operations
c_{1}\xrightarrow{*}c_{2}
. Clearly the unrolling satisfies all the
progressiveness conditions. Consider a corresponding progressive
run-with-id

\tau^{\prime}=d^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{*}d^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{*}d^{\prime}_{2}\xrightarrow{*}d^{\prime}_{3}\cdots
given by Proposition
C.5
.
By Proposition
C.4
, every
id created is either burst or,
\mathsf{seq}_{i}
is infinite with
\mathsf{seq}_{i}

containting an infinite
suffix of trivial deflates. By applying the construction in (S1) iff
(S2),
we replace every id
i

which has an infinite
\mathsf{seq}_{i}
in
\tau^{\prime}
by a
\mathsf{VASS}
token to
obtain a
progressive
\tau^{\prime\prime}
where every balloon is burst. This concludes the
proof of Lemma
5.1
.

C.2.
Proof of Lemma
5.3

The detailed construction of the
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}(T)

corresponding to an
A,B
-witness is given below. The
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}(T)
consists of five total stages broken up into three main stages
and two auxiliary stages.

First Main Stage: Simulating two copies.

We define a
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}_{1}
that simulates two identical copies of the run of
\mathcal{V}
.
The two copies share the global state in
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}_{1}
.
It maintains two copies of the places, one for each run,
and in addition, uses a pair of places
\Omega\times{\{1,2\}}
for
each balloon state in order
to count the total
number of balloons of a given balloon state. An extra pair of places

\Omega\times{\{3,4\}}

for each balloon state remain unused in this stage and are used by
later stages for checking the emptiness of balloons.
Each step of
\mathcal{V}
is simulated by
\mathcal{V}_{1}
by updating two copies of the places and conceptually maintaining
two copies of the balloons.
The only non-trivial point is that we cannot maintain two balloons separately, because two different
steps executing
\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L)
may pick two different contents from
the linear set
L
.
We avoid this by maintaining one balloon with two sets of balloon
places and extend the linear set
L

so that it has two identical copies of the same multiset for each set of places.

We introduce some notation.
For a
v\in{\mathbb{N}}^{P}
(resp.
\delta\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{P}
), we write the “doubling”
v\odot v
(resp.
\delta\odot\delta
)
for the function in
{\mathbb{N}}^{P\times{\{1,2\}}}
such that
v\odot v(p,1)=v\odot v(p,2)=v(p)

(resp.
{\mathbb{Z}}^{P\times{\{1,2\}}}
such that
\delta\odot\delta(p,1)=\delta\odot\delta(p,2)=\delta(p)
).
For a linear set
L\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}^{P}
, we similarly write
L\odot L\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}^{P\times{\{1,2\}}}

for the “doubling” of
L
:

v\odot v\in L\odot L
iff
v\in L
.
A representation of
L\odot L
can be obtained from the representation of
L
by doubling the base and period vectors.
We also write
\mathbf{0}\circ v
(resp.
\mathbf{0}\circ L
) to denote the
extension of
v\in{\mathbb{N}}^{P}
(resp.
L\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}^{P}
)
to
{\mathbb{N}}^{P\times{\{1,2\}}}
: we define
\mathbf{0}\circ v(p,1)=0
and
\mathbf{0}\circ v(p,2)=v(p)
, and
\mathbf{0}\circ v\in\mathbf{0}\circ L

iff
v\in L
.
Finally, we write a function
\delta\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{P}
by explicitly mentioning the components that change.

Formally,

\mathcal{V}_{1}=(Q\cup E\times{\{1,2\}},P\times{\{1,2\}}\cup\Omega\times{\{1,2,3,4\}},\Omega\times\Omega\cup\Omega,\Phi\times{\{1,2\}},E_{1})
,
where we add the following edges to
E_{1}
:

(1)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\delta}q^{\prime}
in
E
there is
q\xrightarrow{\delta\odot\delta}q^{\prime}
in
E_{1}
.

(2)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L)}q^{\prime}
in
E
the edges

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}((\sigma,\sigma),L\odot L)}(e,1)
and
(e,1)\xrightarrow{(\sigma,1)^{+}(\sigma,2)^{+}}q^{\prime}

are in
E_{1}
.
The second edge keeps track of the number of balloons in state
\sigma
.
Note that the first stage only creates balloons with states in
\Omega\times\Omega
.

(3)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
in
E
, the following edges are in
E_{1}
:

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\sigma,\sigma),(\sigma^{\prime},\sigma),(\pi,1),(p,1))}(e,1)
,

(e,1)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\sigma^{\prime},\sigma),(\sigma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}),(\pi,2),(p,2))}(e,2)
,

(e,2)\xrightarrow{(\sigma,1)^{-},(\sigma^{\prime},1)^{+},(\sigma,2)^{-},(\sigma^{\prime},2)^{+}}q^{\prime}
.
The first two edges transfer the balloon tokens to
(p,1)
and
(p,2)
, respectively.
The last edge tracks the new number of balloons in each balloon state.

(4)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)}q^{\prime}
in
E
, the edges
q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}{(\sigma,\sigma)}}(e,1)
and

(e,1)\xrightarrow{(\sigma,1)^{-}(\sigma,2)^{-}}q^{\prime}
are in
E^{\prime}
.
The second edge maintains the count of the number of balloons with balloon state
\sigma
.

For an initial (semi-)configuration
c_{0}=(q_{0},\mathbf{m}_{0},\emptyset)
of
\mathcal{V}
, one can construct a configuration of
\mathcal{V}_{1}
that makes
two identical copies on to the places and initializes the places

\sigma\times{\{1,2,3,4\}}
to zero.
A run of
\mathcal{V}_{1}
decomposes into two identical runs of
\mathcal{V}
, and for any reachable configuration, the places
P\times{\{1\}}
and

P\times{\{2\}}
have identical number of tokens.
So do the places
\Omega\times{\{1\}}
and
\Omega\times{\{2\}}
which
track the number of balloons in each copy for a given balloon state.
The places in
\Omega\times{\{3,4\}}
remain empty.
Each balloon can be divided into two identical balloons by focusing on the two copies of the places.

First Auxilliary Stage: Checking Emptiness of Balloons in
c_{1}

.

The first auxiliary stage uses a
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}

which is used to check balloons in
c_{1}
for emptiness while at the
same time transferring tokens from the copy
\Omega\times{\{1\}}
to
the copy
\Omega\times{\{3\}}
. The only places used in this stage are
those in
\Omega\times{\{1,3\}}
. The states used by
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
are two copies of the states of
\mathcal{V}
together with states of the
form
(q,\sigma,\pi)
used to perform the emptiness check. We also
have two marked copies of balloon states
\tilde{\Omega}\times{\{1,2\}}
. The

\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}

picks a balloon
b
from
c_{1}
and performs
one deflate operation for
each
\pi\in\Phi
on

b
, sending all tokens to a special place
p_{\mathsf{check}}
. During the
check, the balloon is put into the first special marked copy
of its state to
ensure that all of the checks are performed on the balloon
b
picked.
At
the end of the check, a token is transferred indicating that
b
has
been checked for emptiness and the balloon state is put into the
second marked copy. The series of checks forms a loop starting
and ending at a state
(q,1)
while passing through states of the form

(q,\sigma,\pi)
, with one loop per balloon checked. In the event of a
correct check on all
balloons in
c_{1}
, the place
p_{\mathsf{check}}
remains empty, all the
tokens have been transferred and control is passed to the second main
stage. In checking emptiness only the first copy of
Q
is used.
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}

then non-deterministically guesses that all the balloons have been
checked for emptiness and moves from a state
(q,1)
to the state
(q,2)
in the second copy of
Q
. It then converts the balloon states
of all the balloons from the second marked copy to the normal balloon
state. Note that this
conversion cannot be done before all the balloons are checked for
emptiness since we could otherwise make the mistake of checking only
one balloon repeatedly
for emptiness.

Let
\Phi=\{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\cdots,\pi_{n}\}
be an
arbitrary enumeration.
Formally,
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}=(Q_{1\rightarrow 2},p_{\mathsf{check}}\cup\Omega\times{\{1,2,3,4\}},((\Omega\times\Omega)\cup(\tilde{\Omega}\times\tilde{\Omega}\times{\{1,2\}})),\Phi\times{\{1,2\}},E_{1\rightarrow 2})
where
\tilde{\Omega}
is a decorated copy of

\Omega
. The set global of states is
Q_{1\rightarrow 2}=(Q\times{\{1,2\}})\cup(Q\times\Omega\times\Phi)
and
we add the
following edges to
E_{1\rightarrow 2}
for each

q\in Q
and
\sigma\in\Omega
:

(1)

(q,1)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\sigma,\sigma),(\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma},1),(\pi_{1},1),p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,\sigma,\pi_{1})
,

(2)

for each
k\in[1,n-2]
add

(q,\sigma,\pi_{k})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma},1),(\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma},1),(\pi_{k+1},1),p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,\sigma,\pi_{k+1})
,

(3)

(q,\sigma,\pi_{n-1})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma},1),(\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma},2),(\pi_{n},1),p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,\sigma,\pi_{n})
and

(4)

(q,\sigma,\pi_{n})\xrightarrow{(\sigma,1)^{-},(\sigma,3)^{+}}(q,1)
. A token is transferred from
(\Omega,1)
to
(\Omega,3)
.

(5)

(q,1)\xrightarrow{\mathbf{0}}(q,2)
,

(6)

(q,2)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma},2),(\sigma,\sigma),(\pi_{n},1),p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,2)
.

Second Main Stage: Simulating
c_{1}\xrightarrow{*}c_{2}
.

At this point, we note that the first copy of
c_{1}
resides in the
first copy of places and the third copy of balloon states.
The second main stage is given by a
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}_{2}
which keeps the
first copy of
c_{1}
frozen and
simulates the
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}
on the second copy.
It additionally performs verification of the progress constraints.
Thus, a state consists of a triple
(q,q^{\prime},M)\in Q\times Q\times 2^{A\cup B}
, where
q
keeps the state
of
c_{1}
,
q^{\prime}
is the current state of the simulation, and
M
is a
subset of
A\cup B
.
When control moves from state
q
in
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
to
\mathcal{V}_{2}
,
we start at
(q,q,\emptyset)
,
where the
\emptyset
denotes none of the progress constraints have
been checked.

The simulation only updates the second copy of the places.
New balloons now use the states of
\mathcal{V}
and we continue to track the
number of balloons for each balloon state
but only in the second copy i.e. the places
\Omega\times{\{2\}}
. The
other copies
\Omega\times{\{1,3,4\}}
remain untouched.
A
\mathsf{deflate}
or a
\mathsf{burst}
operation may be performed on double-balloons with state
\sigma\times\sigma
or on normal balloons
with state
\sigma
.
On double-balloons,
\mathsf{deflate}
works on the second
component only.

Formally,
\mathcal{V}_{2}=(Q_{2},P\times{\{1,2\}}\cup\Omega\times{\{1,2,3,4\}},\Omega\times\Omega\cup\Omega,\Phi\times{\{1,2\}},E_{2})

consists of states

Q_{2}={\{(q,q^{\prime},M)\mid q\in Q,q^{\prime}\in Q\cup E,M\subseteq A\cup B\}}
, and the following edges in
E_{2}
:

(1)

Let
e=q\xrightarrow{\delta}q^{\prime}
.
Let
P_{\delta}=\{p\in A\mid\delta(p)<0\}
. If
\mathsf{supp}(\delta)\subseteq A
, then
for each
q_{1}\in Q
,
M\subseteq A\cup B
, and
p\in P
, add

(q_{1},q,M)\xrightarrow{\mathbf{0}\circ\delta}(q_{1},q^{\prime},M)
if
p\in P\setminus P_{\delta}
and

(q_{1},q,M)\xrightarrow{}(q_{1},q^{\prime},M\cup P_{\delta})
if
p\in P_{\delta}
.
The
\delta
edges are restricted to those whose support is in
A
. We
track the place progress condition.

(2)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,L)}q^{\prime}
in
E
, for each

q_{1}\in Q
,
M\subseteq A\cup B
, add

(q_{1},q,M)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma,\mathbf{0}\circ L)}(q_{1},e,M)
and

(q_{1},e,M)\xrightarrow{(\sigma,2)^{+}}(q_{1},q^{\prime},M)
.

(3)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
in
E
,
for each
q_{1}\in Q
,
M\subseteq A\cup B
,
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\in\Omega
add:

(q_{1},q,M)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\sigma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),(\sigma^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime}),(\pi,2),(p,2))}(q_{1},e,M^{\prime})
,

(q_{1},q,M)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},(\pi,2),(p,2))}(q_{1},e,M)
,

(q_{1},e,M)\xrightarrow{(\sigma,2)^{-},(\sigma^{\prime},2)^{+}}(q_{1},q^{\prime},M^{\prime})
,
where
M^{\prime}=M\cup{\{\sigma\}}
if
\sigma\in B
and
M^{\prime}=M
otherwise.
The first edge deflates doubled balloons left over from the first main
stage, but only to the second copy of
P
. We
track the balloon progress condition only for balloons from the
first main stage.

(4)

For each
e=q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)}q^{\prime}
in
E
,
for each
q_{1}\in Q
,
M\subseteq A\cup B
,
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Omega
,
add

(q_{1},q,M)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}((\sigma^{\prime},\sigma))}(q_{1},e,M^{\prime})
,

(q_{1},q,M)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)}(q_{1},e,M)
, and

(q_{1},e,M)\xrightarrow{(\sigma,2)^{-}}(q_{1},q^{\prime},M)
,
where
M^{\prime}=M\cup{\{\sigma\}}
if
\sigma\in B
and
M^{\prime}=M
otherwise.

Second Auxiliary Stage: Checking Emptiness of Balloons in

c_{2}
.
The difference between the first auxiliary stage and second auxiliary
stage is that
the emptiness must be checked for both the balloons produced in the
first main stage as well as the second main stage in the latter, since
there may be
a double-balloon
that continues to exist at
c_{2}
. The emptiness check transfers
tokens from the copy
\Omega\times{\{2\}}
to the copy
\Omega\times{\{4\}}
. As in the first auxiliary stage, we
pick an enumeration

{\{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\cdots,\pi_{n}\}}
of
\Phi
.
Formally,
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}=(Q_{2\rightarrow 3},p_{\mathsf{check}}\cup\Omega\times{\{1,2,3,4\}},(\Omega\times\Omega\cup\Omega)\cup((\tilde{\Omega}\times\tilde{\Omega}\cup\tilde{\Omega})\times{\{1,2\}}),\Phi\times{\{1,2\}},E_{2\rightarrow 3})
where
\tilde{\Omega}

is a
decorated
copy of
\Omega
. The set of global states is
Q_{2\rightarrow 3}=(Q\times{\{1,2\}})\cup Q\times\Phi\times(\Omega\cup\Omega\times\Omega)
and
we add the
following edges to
E_{2\rightarrow 3}
for each

q\in Q
,
(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\in\Omega\times\Omega
and
\sigma\in\Omega
:

(1)

(q,1)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}),(\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma^{\prime}},1),\pi_{1},p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,\pi_{1},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
,

q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,(\tilde{\sigma},1),\pi_{1},p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,\pi_{1},\sigma)
,

(2)

for each
k\in[1,n-2]
add

(q,\pi_{k},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma^{\prime}},1),(\tilde{\sigma^{\prime}},\tilde{\sigma^{\prime}},1),\pi_{k+1},p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,\pi_{k+1},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
,

(q,\pi_{k},\sigma)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\tilde{\sigma},1),(\tilde{\sigma},1),\pi_{k+1},p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,\pi_{k+1},\sigma)
,

(3)

(q,\pi_{n-1},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma^{\prime}},1),\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma^{\prime}},2),\pi_{n},p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,\pi_{n},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
,

(q,\pi_{n-1},\sigma)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\tilde{\sigma},1)(\tilde{\sigma},2),\pi_{n},p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,\pi_{n},\sigma)
and

(4)

(q,\pi_{n},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\xrightarrow{(\sigma^{\prime},2)^{-},(\sigma^{\prime},4)^{+}}(q,1)
,

(q,\pi_{n},\sigma)\xrightarrow{(\sigma,2)^{-},(\sigma,4)^{+}}(q,1)

. A token is transferred from
(\Omega,2)
to
(\Omega,4)
.

(5)

(q,1)\xrightarrow{\mathbf{0}}(q,2)
,

(6)

(q,2)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\sigma^{\prime}},2),(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}),\pi_{n},p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,2)
,

(q,2)\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}((\tilde{\sigma},2),\sigma,\pi_{n},p_{\mathsf{check}})}(q,2)
.

Third Main Stage: Verification.

At this point, the first copy of
c_{1}
uses balloon places
\Omega\times{\{3\}}
while
c_{2}
uses the balloon places
\Omega\times{\{4\}}
.
In the third main stage, verification non-deterministically removes
the same number of tokens from the two copies for each place in
A

and for each place in
B
in the copies
\Omega\times{\{3,4\}}
.
Additionally, all balloons are burst.
Finally, all places in
P\times{\{2\}}
and
\Omega\times{\{4\}}
are
emptied.
Formally,
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}_{3}=({\{q_{\mathsf{ver}},q_{f}\}},P\times{\{1,2\}}\cup\Omega\times{\{1,2,3,4\}},\Omega\cup\Omega\times\Omega,\Phi\times{\{1,2\}},E_{3})

contains the following edges:

(1)

For each
p\in A
, there is an edge
q_{\mathsf{ver}}\xrightarrow{(p,1)^{-}(p,2)^{-}}q_{\mathsf{ver}}
and
for each
\sigma\in B
, the edge
q_{\mathsf{ver}}\xrightarrow{(\sigma,3)^{-}(\sigma,4)^{-}}q_{\mathsf{ver}}
.
For each
\sigma\in\Omega
, the edge
q_{\mathsf{ver}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)}q_{\mathsf{ver}}
and for each
(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\in\Omega\times\Omega
the edge
q_{\mathsf{ver}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})}q_{\mathsf{ver}}

(2)

q_{\mathsf{ver}}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{0}}q_{f}
and
for each
p\in A
, the edge
q_{f}\xrightarrow{(p,2)^{-}}q_{f}
and
for each
\sigma\in B
, the edge
q_{f}\xrightarrow{(\sigma,4)^{-}}q_{f}
.

The edges in (1) ensure
\mathsf{p}(c_{1})\leq_{p}\mathsf{p}(c_{2})
in the
places
A\cup B
and that all remaining balloons are removed.
The edges in (2) remove extra tokens in case
\mathsf{p}(c_{1})<_{p}\mathsf{p}(c_{2})
.

Overall
\mathsf{VASSB}
and Transitions between Stages.

The
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}(T)
is a composition of the five stages
\mathcal{V}_{1}
,
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
,
\mathcal{V}_{2}
,
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}
and
\mathcal{V}_{3}
.
A set of glue transitions connect the five stages: these
non-deterministically guess when to move
from
\mathcal{V}_{1}
to
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
,
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
to
\mathcal{V}_{2}
,
\mathcal{V}_{2}
to
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}
and from
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}

to
\mathcal{V}_{3}
. Transfers between stages do not perform any operations and
use the following edges:

•

From
\mathcal{V}_{1}
to
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
, for each
q\in Q
of

\mathcal{V}_{1}
to the corresponding first copy
(q,1)
in
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
,

•

from
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
to
\mathcal{V}_{2}
, for each
(q,2)\in Q\times\{2\}
of
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
to the corresponding
(q,q,\emptyset)
of
\mathcal{V}_{2}
,

•

from
\mathcal{V}_{2}
to
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}
, for each
(q,q,A\cup B)

of
\mathcal{V}_{2}
to the corresponding
(q,1)
of
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}
,
and

•

from
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}
, for each
(q,2)\in Q\times\{2\}
of
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}
to
q_{\mathsf{ver}}
of
\mathcal{V}_{3}
.

Correctness.

A correct simulation of the progressiveness witness results in
\mathcal{V}(T)

reaching
the configuration
(q_{f},\emptyset,\bar{\mathbf{0}})
.
Conversely, suppose
\mathcal{V}(T)
reaches
(q_{f},\emptyset,\bar{\mathbf{0}})
.
This implies that it reaches a configuration
(q_{\mathsf{ver}},\mathbf{m},\nu)

where
\mathbf{m}
only contains tokens in the second copy of
A
and the
fourth copy of

B
. This implies that all the balloons of
c_{2}
were correctly
checked for emptiness in
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}
since no tokens remain
in the second copy of
\Omega
and there are no tokens in
p_{\mathsf{check}}
.
Similarly, the emptiness check performed by
\mathcal{V}_{1\rightarrow 2}
is
also correct.
Thus, the configuration
c_{1}
at the end of
\mathcal{V}_{1}
and
c_{2}
at the end of
\mathcal{V}_{2}

satisfy
\mathsf{p}(c_{1})\leq\mathsf{p}(c_{2})
and they both only contain empty
balloons with state
B

and have non-zero tokens in places in
A
.
Further, the transition between
\mathcal{V}_{2}
and
\mathcal{V}_{2\rightarrow 3}

ensures the progressivenes conditions on
A
and
B
have been
checked.

Appendix D
Proofs for Section
5.3

The proofs of Lemmas
5.4
and
5.5

both assume that the
\mathsf{VASSB}
considered is both zero-base (by Lemma

C.7
) and typed (by Lemma
C.6
).
Further, the notion of runs-with-id introduced in Appendix

C
is used in the proof of Lemma
5.4
.

D.1.
Proof of Lemma
5.4

In any finite run-with-id
\tau
, let
I_{\sigma}(\tau)
be the set of
id’s of non-empty
balloons inflated with state
\sigma
. Note that by definition,
M_{\sigma}.i=0
for any newly created balloon.
Let
\tau^{\prime}=s_{1}\xrightarrow{*}s_{2}=d^{\prime}_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{1}}d^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{2}}d^{\prime}_{2}\cdots
be an arbitrary canonical run-with-id
which is

N
-balloon bounded i.e. inflates at most
N
non-empty balloons. We
equivalently assume that the bound applies to each balloon
state since this implies a bound of
|\Omega|N
on the total number
of balloons. Suppose there exists a state
\sigma_{0}
with
|I_{\sigma_{0}}(\tau^{\prime})|>N
. We will show
that
there exists
\tau=s_{1}\xrightarrow{*}s_{2}
of
\mathcal{V}
with
|I_{\sigma}(\tau)|=|I_{\sigma}(\tau^{\prime})|
for
\sigma\neq\sigma_{0}
and
|I_{\sigma_{0}}(\tau)|<|I_{\sigma_{0}}(\tau^{\prime})|
. Clearly, this suffices to prove the lemma.

For any deflate sequence
S=(\pi_{1},p_{1}),\cdots,(\pi_{n},p_{n})
, let

s(S)
be the set
{\{(\pi_{1},p_{1}),\cdots,(\pi_{n},p_{n})\}}
.
For id’s
i,j\in I_{\sigma_{0}}(\tau^{\prime})
with
i<j
, let
\mathsf{color}_{i,j}:(s(M_{\sigma_{0}}.S))\rightarrow\{\mathsf{green},\mathsf{red}\}
be defined by

\mathsf{color}_{i,j}(\pi,p)=\begin{cases}\mathsf{green}&\text{ if }\exists k<k^{\prime},(\pi,p,k)\in\mathsf{typeseq}_{i},(\pi,p,k^{\prime})\in\mathsf{typeseq}_{j}\\
\mathsf{red}&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

The color
\mathsf{green}
(resp.
\mathsf{red}
) indicates that a deflate
(\pi,p)
of the
balloon
i
occurs before (resp. after) the corresponding deflate of
balloon
j
.
We write
k=\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}(\pi,p)
if
(\pi,p,k)\in\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}
.
The set
\mathcal{C}_{\sigma_{0}}={\{\mathsf{color}_{i,j}\mid i,j\in I_{\sigma_{0}}(\tau^{\prime})\}}
of functions is finite since both the domain and range of
the functions is finite. Let
G_{\sigma_{0}}
be the graph with
I_{\sigma_{0}}

as the set of vertices and edges colored by
\mathcal{C}_{\sigma_{0}}
. Note
that the color
\mathsf{color}_{i,j}
of the edge between vertices
i
and
j

is a finite word from
{\{\mathsf{green},\mathsf{red}\}}^{*}
. Let

r=|\mathcal{C}_{\sigma_{0}}|\leq 2^{|\Phi|}

and
n=|\Omega|^{|\Phi|}+1
. Then by Ramsey’s theorem
(
Ramsey 1930
, Theorem
B)
there exists
R(r;n)\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that for any
r
-colored graph with at least
R(r;n)
vertices, there exists a monochromatic subgraph of size at
least
n
. Further, by the result of Erdős and
Rado
(
Erdős and
Rado 1952
, Theorem 1)
we know that
R(r;n)\leq r^{r(n-2)+1}\leq O(\exp_{4}(|\mathcal{V}|))
. Choosing

N=r^{r(n-2)+1}
, this implies that
|I_{\sigma_{0}}|>r^{r(n-2)+1}
and
therefore there exists a monochromatic subgraph
G^{\prime}
of
G_{\sigma_{0}}

with
at least
n
many vertices. Let
c
be the color of every edge in the
graph induced
by
G^{\prime}
. A

\mathsf{red}
-block

c[i,j]
of
c
is a maximal contiguous
subsequence
(\pi_{i},p_{i}),(\pi_{i+1},p_{i+1}),\cdots,(\pi_{j},p_{j})
of
M_{\sigma_{0}}.S
such
that
c

takes value
\mathsf{red}
on each
(\pi_{k},p_{k})
for
i\leq k\leq j
and
value
\mathsf{green}
on
(\pi_{j+1},p_{j+1})
(if
j<n
) and
(\pi_{i-1},p_{i-1})
(if
i>1
).
Let

c[j_{1,1},j_{1,2}],c[j_{2,1},j_{2,2}],\cdots,c[j_{l,1},j_{l,2}]
be
the
\mathsf{red}
-blocks of
c
with
j_{k,2}<j_{k+1,1}
for each
k
.

Intuitively, deflates of balloons from
G^{\prime}
in a particular red-block
happen in the reverse order as compared to their id’s. That is, if

i_{0}<j_{0}<k_{0}<l_{0}
are id’s which are present in
G^{\prime}
, then in a
red-block,
l_{0}
is deflated first, followed by
k_{0}
, then
j_{0}
and
finally
i_{0}
. In Figure
3
, one such red-block (called

1
-block in the main body) is formed by the second and third deflates
where the magenta outlined balloon corresponding to
l_{0}
is deflated
first at
l_{2}
and
l_{3}
, followed by the green, orange and cyan
outlined
balloons.

Formally, let
\mathsf{min}(G^{\prime})
(resp.
\mathsf{max}(G^{\prime})
) be the
least
(resp. greatest) id in
G^{\prime}
. For each
i\in G^{\prime}
and for each
k

with
1\leq k\leq l
, we have
\mathsf{typeseq}_{\mathsf{max}(G^{\prime})}(j_{k,1})\leq\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}(j_{k,1})
and
\mathsf{typeseq}_{i}(j_{k,2})\leq\mathsf{typeseq}_{\mathsf{min}(G^{\prime})}(j_{k,2})

by construction. In other words, for any
\mathsf{red}
-block
c[j_{k,1},j_{k,2}]
of
c
,
all non-trivial deflates made by balloons in
G^{\prime}
happen in
\tau^{\prime}[m_{k,1},m_{k,2}]
where
m_{k,1}=\mathsf{typeseq}_{\mathsf{max}(G^{\prime})}(j_{k,1})-1
and

m_{k,2}=\mathsf{typeseq}_{\mathsf{min}(G^{\prime})}(j_{k,2})
and further, no other non-trivial deflates of balloons in

G^{\prime}

other than the ones
in
c[j_{k,1},j_{k,2}]
occur in
\tau^{\prime}[m_{k,1},m_{k,2}]
.
Let
\tau^{\prime}[m_{1,1},m_{1,2}],\tau^{\prime}[m_{2,1},m_{2,2}],\cdots,\tau^{\prime}[m_{l,1},m_{l,2}]
be the infixes of
\tau^{\prime}

corresponding to
c[j_{1,1},j_{1,2}],c[j_{2,1},j_{2,2}],\cdots,c[j_{l,1},j_{l,2}]
. We observe that the total number of points
j_{1,1},j_{1,2},j_{2,1},\cdots j_{l,2}
is at most
|\Phi|
since the
\pi
values at all
these points is distinct and therefore, the same bound applies to
m_{1,1},m_{1,2},m_{2,1},m_{2,2},\cdots,m_{l,1},m_{l,2}
. Let
M=\bigcup_{1\leq k\leq l}[m_{k,1},m_{k,2}]
.

By construction,
|G^{\prime}|=n\geq|\Omega|^{|\Phi|}+1
, which implies that there exist
i,j\in G^{\prime}
with
i<j

such that for each
k\geq 1
and each
l\in\{1,2\}
we have
d^{\prime}_{m_{k,l}}.i.\sigma=d^{\prime}_{m_{k,l}}.j.\sigma
i.e. the balloons
i
and
j

have the same balloon state at each of the configurations
d^{\prime}_{m_{k,l}}
. Let
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{i}
(resp.
\mathit{op}^{\prime}_{j}
) in
\tau^{\prime}
create a balloon
(b^{\prime},i)
(resp.
(b^{\prime\prime},j)
) with

b^{\prime}.\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{k}_{i}
(resp.
b^{\prime\prime}.\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{k}_{j}
).
We now
construct
\tau=d_{0}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{1}}d_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathit{op}_{2}}d_{2}\cdots

from
\tau^{\prime}
by an
id-switching surgery
in which all operations
in
\tau
are retained by
\tau^{\prime}
with the exception of those in

\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{i}
and
\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{j}
, which are replaced by
\mathsf{seq}_{i}
and

\mathsf{seq}_{j}

as follows:

(1)

\mathit{op}_{i}
creates
(b_{i},i)
where
b_{i}.\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{k}_{i}+\mathbf{k}_{j}
and
b_{i}.\sigma=b^{\prime}.\sigma

and
\mathit{op}_{j}
creates
(b_{j},j)
where
b_{j}.\mathbf{k}=\emptyset
and

b_{j}.\sigma=b^{\prime\prime}.\sigma
,

(2)

for each
k\in\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{i}
(resp.
k\in\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{j}
) where
k\in M
,

k\in\mathsf{seq}_{j}
(resp.
k\in\mathsf{seq}_{i}
) and

(3)

for each
k\in\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{i}
(resp.
k\in\mathsf{seq}^{\prime}_{j}
) where
k\not\in M
,

k\in\mathsf{seq}_{i}
(resp.
k\in\mathsf{seq}_{j}
).

Intuitively, all operations in
\tau^{\prime}
belonging to the segments in

M

on balloon
i
are reinterpreted as being
performed on balloon
j
in
\tau
and vice versa, while operations
outside of the segments in
M
are retained as before. First we
observe that this reinterpretation results in a valid sequence of
operations
\mathsf{seq}_{i}
and
\mathsf{seq}_{j}
in
\tau
since we have chosen
i,j

such that the balloon states match at every boundary point of
M
.
This implies
that
i<_{t}j
in
\tau
and hence (1) which is a token-shifting
operation ensures that
\tau
is valid by Proposition

C.8
and is a run from
s_{1}
to
s_{2}
. Since
balloon
j
is empty in
\tau
, this implies
|I_{\sigma_{0}}(\tau)|<|I_{\sigma_{0}}(\tau^{\prime})|
.

D.2.
Proof of Lemma
5.5

Given
\mathsf{VASSB}

\mathcal{V}=(Q,P,{\Omega},{\Phi},E)
and its semiconfigurations
s_{1},s_{2}
, we
construct a
\mathsf{VASS}

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=(Q^{\prime},P^{\prime},E^{\prime})
and its configurations

s^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{2}
such
that there exists an
N
-balloon-bounded run
\rho=s_{1}\xrightarrow{*}s_{2}
of
\mathcal{V}
(where
N
is given by Lemma
5.4
) iff
(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},s^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{2})\in\mathsf{REACH}
.

We assume that
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
can have edges of the form
e=q\xrightarrow{+\Delta_{L}}q^{\prime}
where
+\Delta_{L}
is an operation which non-determinstically adds
an element from a
linear
set
L
to the set of places, since reducing such an extension to a
normal VASS
is a standard construction. We similarly have operations

-\Delta_{L}
which remove a vector
\mathbf{v}\in L
from the
set
of
places.
For sets
\Phi=\{\pi_{1},\cdots,\pi_{n}\}
and
\Phi^{\prime}=\{\pi^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\pi^{\prime}_{n}\}
, for

L\subseteq{\mathbb{M}[\Phi]}
, we write
L(\Phi\leftarrow\Phi^{\prime})\subseteq{\mathbb{M}[\Phi^{\prime}]}
for the corresponding linear set of vectors

\mathbf{v}^{\prime}

where

\mathbf{v}^{\prime}(\pi^{\prime}_{i})=\mathbf{v}(\pi_{i})
for each
i
where
\mathbf{v}\in L
.
For
L\subseteq{\mathbb{M}[{\{\pi\}}]}
and
p\in P
, we also define
the multiset
L(\pi\odot(-p))\subseteq{\mathbb{M}[{\{\pi,p\}}]}

consisting of vectors
\mathbf{v}^{-}\odot\mathbf{v}(p)
obtained from
\mathbf{v}\in L
as
(\mathbf{v}^{-}\odot\mathbf{v}(p))(\pi)=-(\mathbf{v}^{-}\odot\mathbf{v}(p))(p)=-\mathbf{v}(\pi)
.
The projection
L~\hskip-2.84544pt\downharpoonright_{\chi}
of
L
to
a subset
\chi\subseteq\Phi
is obtained by retaining the values of vectors for the

\chi
component and setting the rest to 0. All of the above defined
operations enable us to simulate balloon operations by addition and
subtraction operations on
\mathsf{VASS}
places.

Let
H
be the set of all functions
\eta:\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}\rightarrow\Omega\cup{\{\#,\dagger\}}
. A function

\eta\in H
is used in the following construction to track the state
of a non-empty balloon of
\mathcal{V}
in the global state of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
. The

\#
symbol indicates that a balloon
has non
yet been inflated and
\dagger
indicates that it has been burst. Let

G
be
the set of vectors
\mathbf{w}:\Omega\rightarrow{\{0,1,\cdots,N\}}
.
A
vector
\mathbf{w}\in G
is used to store the number of non-empty balloons
of a particular balloon state in
\mathcal{V}
in the global state of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.
Increments
p^{+}
(resp. decrements
p^{-}
) to a place
p
are used as
before. For a type sequence
S=(\pi_{1},p_{1}),\cdots,(\pi_{n},p_{n})
,
we write
\mathsf{set}_{1}(S)
for the set
{\{\pi_{1},\cdots,\pi_{n}\}}
.

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
is defined as:

•

Q^{\prime}=Q\times G\times H\cup{\{q_{f}\}}
,

•

P^{\prime}=P\cup\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}\times\Phi\cup\Omega
,

•

E^{\prime}
contains the following edges:

(1)

for each
q\xrightarrow{\delta}q^{\prime}
in
E
, for each
\mathbf{w}\in G
and
\eta\in H
add

(q,\mathbf{w},\eta)\xrightarrow{\delta}(q^{\prime},\mathbf{w},\eta)
to
E^{\prime}
,

(2)

for each
q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{inflate}(\sigma_{1},L)}q^{\prime}
in
E
,

(a)

for each
\mathbf{w}\in G
and
\eta\in H
add
(q,\mathbf{w},\eta)\xrightarrow{\sigma_{1}^{+}}(q^{\prime},\mathbf{w},\eta)
to
E^{\prime}
,

(b)

for each
\eta\in H
and for each
\mathbf{w}\in G
such that

\mathbf{w}(\sigma_{1})<N
, add

(q,\mathbf{w},\eta)\xrightarrow{\Delta_{\mathbf{w},\sigma_{1}}}(q^{\prime},\mathbf{w}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})
to
E^{\prime}

where

\Delta_{\mathbf{w},\sigma_{1}}
adds a vector from
L_{\sigma_{1}}(\Phi\leftarrow\{\sigma_{1}\}\times\{\mathbf{w}(\sigma_{1})+1\}\times\Phi)
,

the vector

\mathbf{w}^{\prime}
satisfies
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}(\sigma_{1})=\mathbf{w}(\sigma_{1})+1
and

\mathbf{w}^{\prime}(\sigma)=\mathbf{w}(\sigma)
for
\sigma\neq\sigma_{1}
, and

\eta^{\prime}
satisfies
\eta^{\prime}(\sigma_{1},\mathbf{w}(\sigma_{1})+1)=\sigma_{1}
and
for all
(\sigma,i)\neq(\sigma_{1},\mathbf{w}(\sigma_{1})+1)
we have

\eta^{\prime}(\sigma,i)=\eta(\sigma,i)
,

(3)

for each
q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{deflate}(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime},\pi,p)}q^{\prime}
in

E
,

(a)

for each
\mathbf{w}\in G
and
\eta\in H
add
(q,\mathbf{w},\eta)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{\prime+},\sigma^{-}}(q^{\prime},\mathbf{w},\eta)
to
E^{\prime}
,

(b)

for each
\mathbf{w}\in G
, for each
\eta\in H
and
(\sigma_{1},i)\in\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}
such that
\eta(\sigma_{1},i)=\sigma
,

(i)

if
M_{\sigma^{\prime}}.i=M_{\sigma}.i+1
then add

(q,\mathbf{w},\eta)\xrightarrow{\Delta_{\sigma_{1},i,\pi,p}}(q^{\prime},\mathbf{w},\eta^{\prime})

to
E^{\prime}

where

\Delta_{\sigma_{1},i,\pi,p}
adds a vector
\mathbf{v}^{-}\odot\mathbf{v}(p)
corresponding to some
\mathbf{v}\in L_{\sigma_{1}}~\hskip-5.69046pt\downharpoonright_{\pi}(\pi\leftarrow\{\sigma_{1}\}\times\{i\}\times\pi)

and

\eta^{\prime}
satisfies
\eta^{\prime}(\sigma_{1},i)=\sigma^{\prime}
and
\eta^{\prime}(\sigma,j)=\eta(\sigma,j)
for all
(\sigma,j)\neq(\sigma_{1},i)
,

(ii)

else add

(q,\mathbf{w},\eta)\xrightarrow{\mathbf{0}}(q^{\prime},\mathbf{w},\eta^{\prime})

to
E^{\prime}

where

\eta^{\prime}
satisfies
\eta^{\prime}(\sigma_{1},i)=\sigma^{\prime}
and
\eta^{\prime}(\sigma_{2},j)=\eta(\sigma_{2},j)
for all
(\sigma_{2},j)\neq(\sigma_{1},i)
,

(4)

for each
q\xrightarrow{\mathsf{burst}(\sigma)}q^{\prime}
in

E
,

(a)

for each
\mathbf{w}\in G
and
\eta\in H
add
(q,\mathbf{w},\eta)\xrightarrow{\sigma^{-}}(q^{\prime},\mathbf{w},\eta)
to
E^{\prime}
,

(b)

for each
\mathbf{w}\in G
, for each
\eta\in H
and
(\sigma_{1},i)\in\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}
such that
\eta(\sigma_{1},i)=\sigma
, add

(q,\mathbf{w},\eta)\xrightarrow{-\Delta_{\sigma_{1},i,(\Phi\setminus\mathsf{set}_{1}(M_{\sigma_{1}}.S))}}(q^{\prime},\mathbf{w},\eta^{\prime})
to
E^{\prime}

where

\Delta_{\sigma_{1},i,(\Phi\setminus\mathsf{set}_{1}(M_{\sigma_{1}}.S))}
adds
a
vector from
L_{\sigma_{1}}~\hskip-5.69046pt\downharpoonright_{(\Phi\setminus\mathsf{set}_{1}(M_{\sigma_{1}}.S))}((\Phi\setminus\mathsf{set}_{1}(M_{\sigma_{1}}.S))\leftarrow\{\sigma_{1}\}\times\{i\}\times(\Phi\setminus\mathsf{set}_{1}(M_{\sigma_{1}}.S)))

and

\eta^{\prime}
satisfies
\eta^{\prime}(\sigma_{1},i)=\dagger
and
\eta^{\prime}(\sigma_{2},j)=\eta(\sigma_{2},j)
for all
(\sigma_{2},j)\neq(\sigma_{1},i)
,

(5)

for each
\eta
such that for all
(\sigma,i)\in\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}
we have
\eta(\sigma,i)=\#
or
\eta(\sigma,i)=\dagger
, add

(s_{2}.q,\mathbf{w},\eta)\xrightarrow{\mathbf{0}}q_{f}
.

The configuration
s^{\prime}_{1}
is given as
s^{\prime}_{1}.q=(s_{1}.q,\mathbf{0},\eta_{0})

where
\eta_{0}(\sigma,i)=\#
for all
(\sigma,i)\in\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}
, and
s^{\prime}_{1}.\mathbf{m}(p)=s_{1}.\mathbf{m}(p)
for
p\in P
and
s^{\prime}_{1}.\mathbf{m}(p)=0
otherwise. The
configuration
s^{\prime}_{2}
is given as
s^{\prime}_{2}.q=q_{f}
, and
s^{\prime}_{2}.\mathbf{m}(p)=s_{2}.\mathbf{m}(p)
for
p\in P
and
s^{\prime}_{2}.\mathbf{m}(p)=0
otherwise.

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
contains extra places
\Omega
which are used to store the number
of balloons of a given state
\sigma
which were created empty and
remain empty throughout a run, as well as places
\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}\times\Phi

which are used to store the contents of non-empty balloons created
during an
N
-balloon-bounded run of
\mathcal{V}
. The
G
-component of the
global state is
used to track the total number of non-empty balloons created while the

H
-component tracks the state changes of every balloon which was
created non-empty. The initial configuration
s^{\prime}_{1}
of
\mathcal{V}
contains

s_{1}
in its
P
-places while other places remain empty. The

G
-component of the global state is set to the vector
\mathbf{0}
since
no non-empty balloons have been created and the
H
-component is set
to the function
\eta_{0}
which is the constant function taking value

\#
. The edges in (1) are used to simulate the place edges of
\mathcal{V}

and hence do not modify either the
G
or the
H
-component.
Similarly, the simulation of operations on balloons which were created
empty use edges from
(2a),(3a)
and
(4a)
which also do not
modify either the
G
or the
H
-component and simply increment or
decrement places from
\Omega
as appropriate.

An inflate
balloon operation in the run
\rho
of
\mathcal{V}
which creates the
i^{th}

non-empty balloon (upto the bound
N
) with state
\sigma
is simulated
by using edges
from
(2b)
which
increment
\mathbf{w}\in G
from
i-1
to
i
and set the value of
\eta(\sigma,i)
to
\sigma
from its original value of
\#
, while
simultaneously populating the places in
(\sigma,i,\Phi)
with a
vector from
L_{\sigma}
. Deflate operations on non-empty balloons
similarly are simulated by updating
\eta
and removing tokens from
the appropriate place using the edges in
(3b)
. Here, we make a
distinction between the first time that a place
\pi
undergoes a
deflate, in which case
(3bi)
is used and for every later deflate
on the place
\pi
, we use
(3bii)
which does not transfer any
tokens. This implies that that for every non-empty balloon created,
there is only one opportunity for
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
to transfer tokens away from
places in
\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}\times\Phi
.

The edges in
(4b)
simulate a burst operation by allowing the
removal of tokens from places which have not been transferred away,
while setting the value of
\eta
to
\dagger
at the appropriate
place to indicate that the balloon has been burst.

Finally, we only allow a move to the state
q_{f}
if the
H
-component
indicates that all non-empty balloons produced have been burst.
A correct simulation ensures that no tokens remain in any of the
places
\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}\times\Phi\cup\Omega
. Thus

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
can reach
s^{\prime}_{2}
from
s^{\prime}_{1}
if
\mathcal{V}
can reach
s_{2}

from
s_{1}
.
Conversely, we note that the sequence of deflates on each non-empty
balloon is tracked in the global state and as mentioned,
there is only one opportunity for
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
to correctly transfer tokens
from the places
\Omega\times{\{1,\cdots,N\}}\times\Phi
for each
non-empty balloon. Thus, from any run
s^{\prime}_{1}\xrightarrow{*}s^{\prime}_{2}
of

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
we obtain a run
s_{1}\xrightarrow{*}s_{2}
of
\mathcal{V}
.

Appendix E
Proofs from Section
6

E.1.
Proof of Lemma
6.1

See
6.1

Proof.

Clearly, a starving run of a DCPS has to be consistent. Suppose a
DCPS has a consistent run
\rho
. Then there are configurations

c_{1},c_{2},\ldots
and thread executions
e_{1},e_{2},\ldots
that produce

\mathbf{m}_{1},\mathbf{m}_{2},\ldots
so that the consistency conditions are met.
In particular, for
V_{p}=\{\mathbf{m}_{j}\mid\text{$e_{j}$ has type
$t$}\}
, the tuple
\mathfrak{V}=(V_{t})_{t\in\mathcal{T}}
is

\mathfrak{S}_{\mathcal{A}}
-consistent. This means, there exists a stack content
w\in\Gamma^{*}
such that
we can choose for each
j\geq 1
, a
thread execution
e^{\prime}_{j}
(not necessarily occurring in
\rho
) that produces a multiset
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}_{j}
such that:

(1)

e^{\prime}_{j}
has the same type as
e_{j}

(2)

\mathbf{m}_{j}\leq_{1}\mathbf{m}^{\prime}_{j}
, and

(3)

e^{\prime}_{j}
arrives in
w
after executing
i
segments.

The idea is now to replace executions
e_{j}
with
e^{\prime}_{j}
to obtain a
starving run. Replacing any individual
e_{j}
by
e^{\prime}_{j}
would yield
an infinite run, because
e^{\prime}_{j}
has the same type (so that state
transition would still be possible), and
\mathbf{m}^{\prime}_{j}\geq_{1}\mathbf{m}_{j}
, so
that the bag contents are supersets of the bag contents of the old
run.

However, the resulting run might not be progressive:
e^{\prime}_{j}
might
spawn an additional thread
(\gamma,0)
such that
(\gamma,0)

becomes active only finitely many times during
\rho
. In that case,
the extra
(\gamma,0)
would be in the bag forever, but never
scheduled.

To remedy this, we only replace all but finitely many of the

e_{j}
. Let
